The cache shipped\n# with the dataset is pre-dated so it survives; this keeps what this run caches alive too.\nREST_EXPIRY = datetime.timedelta(days=365 * 50)\n\nT_START, T_STOP = -0.5, 1.5      # decoding window around stimulus onset, following the reference code\nBIN = 0.02\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\nEDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nTIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # bin centres, the first decoder input\n\n# reaction time bounds of the paper, which truncates the distribution at 80 ms and 2 s;\nMIN_RT, MAX_RT = 0.08, 2.0\n\nQC_LABEL = 1.0                   # the \"stringent quality control\" of the paper, 75,708 units of 621,733\nSPLIT = [100 / 3, 2 * 100 / 3]       # equal sized classes, from percentiles of each session's own trace\n\nCHOICE = {1.0: 0, -1.0: 1}       # +1 is a leftward choice, -1 rightward; 0 is a no response, dropped\nPRIOR = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\nMOVEMENT = ['low', 'medium', 'high']\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_stimulus_onset', 'trial_number_in_block']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'prior_probability_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [['left', 'right'], ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'], MOVEMENT, MOVEMENT]\n\n# the cache location is not known until --datadir is parsed, and the connection cannot be\n# shared across a fork anyway, so connect() fills this in per process rather than at import\none = None\nsubset_csv = None    # DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv path, or None on the full dataset; set by connect()\nbrain_regions = BrainRegions()\n\n\ndef connect(datadir):\n    \"\"\"Point the module-level ONE client at the cache under datadir.\n\n    An Alyx-backed client is required: eid2pid, which load_neural() uses to find the probe\n    insertions, exists only on OneAlyx. mode='local' returns a plain One without it. The\n    client also resolves each dataset's current revision, which is what is on disk -- the\n    release tables list the pre-revision paths, so resolving from them alone finds no\n    trials. The metadata that resolution needs is cached under one_cache/.rest, so a run\n    does not have to reach Alyx.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder.\n    Sets the globals `one` and `subset_csv`; returns nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    global one\n    global subset_csv\n    cache = os.path.join(datadir, ONE_CACHE_DIRNAME)\n    tables = os.path.join(cache, RELEASE)\n    client = dict(base_url=BASE_URL, silent=True, cache_dir=cache, tables_dir=tables,\n                  mode='remote')\n    try:\n        # No password: passing one forces re-authentication over the network, while the\n        # token the fetch cached needs no connection.\n        one = ONE(**client)\n    except Exception:\n        one = ONE(password=PASSWORD, **client)\n    one.alyx.default_expiry = REST_EXPIRY\n    # One.load_cache reads the parquet tables where they are; the OneAlyx override would\n    # ask Alyx for a newer copy first. In 'remote' mode nothing is loaded until this is\n    # called, and search() then returns no sessions at all, without raising.\n    One.load_cache(one, tables_dir=tables)\n    # Only the datalimit dataset ships this file; on the full dataset there is nothing to\n    # restrict to, and subset_csv stays None.\n    path = os.path.join(datadir, 'DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv')\n    subset_csv = path if os.path.isfile(path) else None\n\n\n# --- sessions ---\n\ndef select_sessions():\n    \"\"\"Released sessions that have everything the decoder needs, grouped by subject.\"\"\"\n\n    required = ['spikes.times.npy', 'spikes.clusters.npy',          # ephys\n                '_ibl_trials.table.pqt',                            # task\n                '_ibl_wheel.position.npy', '_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy']\n\n    keep = set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=required))\n    keep &= (set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=['leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n                                                          '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy']))\n             | set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=['rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n                                                            '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy'])))\n    # The datalimit dataset ships a subset of the release (see DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv). The ONE\n    # release index is unmodified and still lists every released session, so\n    # restrict to the eids named there -- otherwise a session that is not on disk\n    # would be fetched over the network. The full dataset has no such file, and every\n    # session the index lists is on disk.\n    # Compare in string space: one.search returns UUID objects while the csv column\n    # is str, so a plain set intersection is empty whatever the contents. keep must\n    # stay UUID-valued for the `eid in keep` test below.\n    if subset_csv is not None:\n        subset_eids = set(pd.read_csv(subset_csv).eid.astype(str))\n        keep = {eid for eid in keep if str(eid) in subset_eids}\n        if not keep:\n            raise SystemExit(f'No sessions matched {subset_csv}; refusing to continue.')\n\n    eids, info = one.search(query_type='local', details=True)\n    selected = {}\n    for eid, session in zip(eids, info):\n        if eid in keep:\n            selected.setdefault(session['subject'], []).append(str(eid))\n    return {subject: selected[subject] for subject in sorted(selected)}\n\n\ndef camera_view(eid):\n    \"\"\"The side camera this session has whisker motion energy for, left preferred.\"\"\"\n    for view in ('left', 'right'):\n        if len(one.list_datasets(eid, filename=f'{view}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n                                 query_type='local')):\n            return view\n    raise ValueError(f'no whisker motion energy for {eid}')\n\n\n# --- trials ---\n\ndef trial_variables(trials):\n    \"\"\"Choice, block prior and trial number in block, one row per trial.\"\"\"\n    # probabilityLeft is constant within a block, so a change of value starts a new block\n    block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n\n    return pd.DataFrame({'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n                         'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n                         'trial_in_block': trials.groupby(block).cumcount()})\n\n\ndef covered(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Trials whose window is spanned by this stream, allowing the one bin gap the reference allows.\"\"\"\n    start, stop = stim_on + T_START, stim_on + T_STOP\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, start, side='right')      # first sample inside the window\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, stop, side='left') - 1       # last sample inside the window\n\n    # a trial with no sample at all, or a gap at either edge, has no usable trace\n    inside = begin <= end\n    first = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\n    last = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\n    return inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n\n\ndef usable_trials(trials, wheel_times, camera_times):\n    \"\"\"Trials the paper would keep, that also have a full wheel and camera window.\"\"\"\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n    reaction = trials.firstMovement_times.to_numpy() - stim_on\n\n    keep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\n    keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n\n    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n\n\n# --- behavior ---\n\ndef window_slice(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n\n    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n\n    return begin, end\n\n\ndef trial_traces(times, values, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"A continuous trace resampled onto the same time grid for every trial.\"\"\"\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    traces = []\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        # one sample either side of the window, so the first and last bin centre are\n        # interpolated rather than clamped to the nearest sample inside it\n        first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n        last = end[trial] + 1\n\n        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n\n    return np.array(traces)\n\n\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n\n\n# --- neural ---\n\ndef load_neural(eid):\n    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n    for pid, name in zip(*one.eid2pid(eid)):\n        loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n\n        # skip the md5 check, which rereads every spike file and draws its own progress bar\n        spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n\n        # a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\n        if not clusters:\n            continue\n\n        clusters = loader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels)   # attaches metrics and histology\n\n        # `void` is the atlas label for a channel the histology placed outside the brain,\n        # so a unit there is not in a brain region at all\n        in_brain = brain_regions.acronym2acronym(\n            clusters['acronym'], mapping='Beryl') != 'void'\n\n        # spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n        # can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n        is_good = (clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL) & in_brain\n        keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n\n        # renumber the surviving clusters, continuing the count from the previous probe\n        new_index = np.cumsum(is_good) - 1\n        units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n        times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n\n        # Beryl is the coarser atlas the reference code uses, e.g. DG-sg and DG-mo both become DG\n        regions.extend(brain_regions.acronym2acronym(clusters['acronym'][is_good], mapping='Beryl'))\n\n    times, units = np.concatenate(times), np.concatenate(units)\n\n    # sort the merged probes by spike time, as the reference does, so trials can be sliced\n    order = np.argsort(times, kind='stable')\n    return times[order], units[order], np.array(regions)\n\n\ndef spike_counts(times, units, n_units, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Spike count per unit and bin, for every trial.\"\"\"\n    counts = np.empty((stim_on.size, n_units, N_BINS))\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        spike_time = times[begin[trial]:end[trial]] - onset      # time from stimulus onset\n        spike_unit = units[begin[trial]:end[trial]]\n\n        bin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n        \n        # a spike sitting on the window edge can round out of range once the onset is subtracted\n        bin_index = np.clip(bin_index, 0, N_BINS - 1)\n\n        # one flat index per spike lets a single bincount fill the whole unit by bin grid\n        flat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\n        counts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n\n    return counts\n\n\n# --- session ---\n\ndef process_session(eid, subject):\n    \"\"\"One session as lists of per trial neural, input and output arrays.\"\"\"\n    loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n    loader.load_trials()\n    loader.load_wheel()\n    view = camera_view(eid)\n    loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n\n    trials, wheel = loader.trials, loader.wheel\n    motion_energy = loader.motion_energy[f'{view}Camera']\n\n    variables = trial_variables(trials)                 # (`/app/code/code_zhang2025`,\n`src/0_data_caching.py` + `src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`) and the inclusion criteria of the IBL\nBrain-Wide Map paper:\n\n  * trials aligned to `stimOn_times`, window (-0.5, +1.5) s, 20 ms bins  -> T = 100\n  * probes of a session merged into one population\n  * only well-isolated units (IBL unit QC `label == 1`) in grey matter (Beryl region not void/root)\n  * trials filtered with the reference `load_trials_and_mask` criteria\n  * wheel speed = |wheel velocity|, whisker motion energy from the left camera (right as fallback),\n    linearly interpolated onto the trial bin grid exactly as `get_behavior_per_interval` does\n\nUsage:\n    python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--show-processing]\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport time\nimport traceback\nimport multiprocessing as mp\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Parameters -- identical to the reference `params` in src/0_data_caching.py\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nALIGN_TIME = 'stimOn_times'\nTIME_WINDOW = (-0.5, 1.5)          # seconds relative to the alignment event\nBINSIZE = 0.02                     # seconds\nNBINS = int(np.ceil((TIME_WINDOW[1] - TIME_WINDOW[0]) / BINSIZE))   # 100\n\nMIN_RT, MAX_RT = 0.08, 2.0         # reference load_trials_and_mask defaults\nMAX_TRIAL_LEN = 10.0               # reference prepare_data passes max_trial_len=10.0\nNAN_EXCLUDE = ['stimOn_times', 'choice', 'feedback_times',\n               'probabilityLeft', 'firstMovement_times', 'feedbackType']\n\nQC_LABEL = 1.0                     # IBL unit QC: 1 == passes all three RIGOR single-unit metrics\nEXCLUDE_REGIONS = ('void', 'root')  # not grey matter / outside the brain\nMIN_NEURONS = 5      # BWM paper: analyses require >= 5 well-isolated neurons\nMIN_TRIALS = 2\n\nBWM_RELEASE_CSV = '/app/code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv'\nONE_BASE_URL = 'https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org'\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_stim_on', 'trial_number_in_block']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'prior_prob_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    ['left', 'right'],\n    ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'],\n    ['low', 'medium', 'high'],\n    ['low', 'medium', 'high'],\n]\n\n\ndef get_one():\n    \"\"\"ONE instance backed by the staged local cache (works fully offline).\"\"\"\n    from one.api import ONE\n    return ONE(base_url=ONE_BASE_URL, mode='remote')\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Trial curation -- copy of reference `load_trials_and_mask` query (ibl_data_utils.py)\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef compute_trials_mask(trials, min_rt=MIN_RT, max_rt=MAX_RT, max_trial_len=MAX_TRIAL_LEN,\n                        nan_exclude=NAN_EXCLUDE, exclude_nochoice=True):\n    query = f'(firstMovement_times - stimOn_times < {min_rt})'\n    query += f' | (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times > {max_rt})'\n    if max_trial_len is not None:\n        query += f' | (feedback_times - goCue_times > {max_trial_len})'\n    for event in nan_exclude:\n        query += f' | {event}.isnull()'\n    if exclude_nochoice:\n        query += ' | (choice == 0)'\n    return ~trials.eval(query)\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Neural binning -- equivalent to reference bin_spiking_data / bincount2D\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef bin_spikes(spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, align_times,\n               t_start=TIME_WINDOW[0], t_end=TIME_WINDOW[1], binsize=BINSIZE, n_bins=NBINS):\n    \"\"\"Spike counts in (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins).\n\n    Bin j of a trial covers [align + t_start + j*binsize, align + t_start + (j+1)*binsize),\n    the same half-open convention as `iblutil.numerical.bincount2D(xlim=[t_beg, t_end])`.\n    `spike_clusters` must already be re-indexed to 0..n_clusters-1.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(align_times)\n    out = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n    begs = align_times + t_start\n    ends = align_times + t_end\n    i0 = np.searchsorted(spike_times, begs, side='left')\n    i1 = np.searchsorted(spike_times, ends, side='left')\n    for k in range(n_trials):\n        s, e = i0[k], i1[k]\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n        b = ((spike_times[s:e] - begs[k]) / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n        np.clip(b, 0, n_bins - 1, out=b)\n        idx = spike_clusters[s:e].astype(np.int64) * n_bins + b\n        cnt = np.bincount(idx, minlength=n_clusters * n_bins)\n        out[k] = cnt.reshape(n_clusters, n_bins)\n    return out\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Behaviour binning -- equivalent to reference get_behavior_per_interval\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef bin_behavior(times, values, align_times, t_start=TIME_WINDOW[0], t_end=TIME_WINDOW[1],\n                 binsize=BINSIZE, n_bins=NBINS):\n    \"\"\"Interpolate a continuous behavioural signal onto the trial bin grid.\n\n    Returns (n_trials, n_bins) array and a boolean validity mask.  As in the reference, the\n    interpolation grid is `np.linspace(beg + binsize, end, n_bins)` (the right edge of every bin)\n    and a trial is invalid if the signal does not cover the window (starts too late / ends too\n    early, by more than one bin) or contains NaNs inside the window.\n    \"\"\"\n    begs = align_times + t_start\n    ends = align_times + t_end\n    n_trials = len(align_times)\n    out = np.zeros((n_trials, n_bins), dtype=np.float64)\n    valid = np.ones(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n\n    idx_beg = np.searchsorted(times, begs, side='right')\n    idx_end = np.searchsorted(times, ends, side='left')\n\n    grid = (np.linspace(binsize, t_end - t_start, n_bins)[None, :] + begs[:, None])\n    for k in range(n_trials):\n        ib, ie = idx_beg[k], idx_end[k]\n        if ie <= ib:\n            valid[k] = False\n            continue\n        tt = times[ib:ie]\n        vv = values[ib:ie]\n        if np.abs(begs[k] - tt[0]) > binsize or np.abs(ends[k] - tt[-1]) > binsize:\n            valid[k] = False      # target data starts too late / ends too early\n            continue\n        if np.any(np.isnan(vv)):\n            valid[k] = False      # NaNs would break the decoder\n            continue\n        out[k] = np.interp(grid[k], tt, vv)\n    return out, valid\n\n\ndef discretize_tertiles(x, valid_rows):\n    \"\"\"Discretize a (n_trials, n_bins) signal into 3 classes using per-session tertiles.\"\"\"\n    ref = x[valid_rows].ravel()\n    q1, q2 = np.quantile(ref, [1. / 3., 2. / 3.])\n    lab = np.zeros(x.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n    lab[x > q1] = 1\n    lab[x > q2] = 2\n    return lab, (float(q1), float(q2))\n\n\ndef trial_number_in_block(prob_left):\n    \"\"\"0-based index of each trial within its block, computed on the raw trial sequence.\"\"\"\n    pl = np.asarray(prob_left, dtype=float)\n    newblock = np.ones(len(pl), dtype=bool)\n    newblock[1:] = pl[1:] != pl[:-1]\n    idx = np.zeros(len(pl), dtype=np.int64)\n    c = 0\n    for i in range(len(pl)):\n        c = 0 if newblock[i] else c + 1\n        idx[i] = c\n    return idx\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-session conversion\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef convert_session(eid, show_processing=False, outdir='/app'):\n    \"\"\"Convert one session; returns a dict or None if the session is unusable.\"\"\"\n    from brainbox.io.one import SessionLoader, SpikeSortingLoader\n    from iblatlas.regions import BrainRegions\n\n    t_start_session = time.time()\n    timing = {}\n    one = get_one()\n    br = BrainRegions()\n\n    # ---------------- trials ----------------\n    t0 = time.time()\n    sl = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n    sl.load_trials()\n    trials = sl.trials\n    mask = compute_trials_mask(trials).to_numpy()\n    timing['trials'] = time.time() - t0\n    if mask.sum() < MIN_TRIALS:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='too few trials after mask')\n\n    # ---------------- spikes ----------------\n    t0 = time.time()\n    pids, pnames = one.eid2pid(eid)\n    spike_times_l, spike_clu_l, acronyms_l, labels_l = [], [], [], []\n    probe_t0, probe_t1 = [], []          # spike-sorted recording coverage per probe\n    offset = 0\n    for pid, pname in zip(pids, pnames):\n        ssl = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=str(pid), one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\n        spikes, clusters, channels = ssl.load_spike_sorting()\n        clu = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels).to_df()\n        # merge probes as in reference `merge_probes`: offset cluster ids so they stay unique\n        probe_t0.append(np.nanmin(spikes['times']))\n        probe_t1.append(np.nanmax(spikes['times']))\n        spike_times_l.append(spikes['times'])\n        spike_clu_l.append(spikes['clusters'] + offset)\n        acronyms_l.append(clu['acronym'].to_numpy())\n        labels_l.append(clu['label'].to_numpy())\n        offset += int(clu.index.max()) + 1\n    spike_times = np.concatenate(spike_times_l)\n    spike_clusters = np.concatenate(spike_clu_l)\n    acronyms = np.concatenate(acronyms_l)\n    labels = np.concatenate(labels_l)\n    srt = np.argsort(spike_times, kind='stable')\n    spike_times = spike_times[srt]\n    spike_clusters = spike_clusters[srt]\n    timing['spikes'] = time.time() - t0\n\n    # ---------------- neuron curation ----------------\n    beryl = br.acronym2acronym(acronyms, mapping='Beryl')\n    keep = (labels >= QC_LABEL) & (~np.isin(beryl, EXCLUDE_REGIONS))\n    keep_ids = np.where(keep)[0]\n    if len(keep_ids) < MIN_NEURONS:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='too few good neurons')\n    remap = -np.ones(offset, dtype=np.int64)\n    remap[keep_ids] = np.arange(len(keep_ids))\n    sel = np.isin(spike_clusters, keep_ids)\n    sp_t = spike_times[sel]\n    sp_c = remap[spike_clusters[sel]]\n    regions = beryl[keep_ids]\n\n    # ---------------- behaviour ----------------\n    t0 = time.time()\n    sl.load_wheel()\n    wheel_times = sl.wheel['times'].to_numpy()\n    wheel_speed_raw = np.abs(sl.wheel['velocity'].to_numpy())\n\n    me_times = me_vals = None\n    camera_used = None\n    for view, key in (('left', 'leftCamera'), ('right', 'rightCamera')):\n        try:\n            sl.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n            df = sl.motion_energy[key]\n            me_times = df['times'].to_numpy()\n            me_vals = df['whiskerMotionEnergy'].to_numpy()\n            camera_used = view\n            break\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n    if me_times is None:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='no whisker motion energy')\n    timing['behavior_load'] = time.time() - t0\n\n    # ---------------- bin everything on the masked trials ----------------\n    t0 = time.time()\n    align_all = trials[ALIGN_TIME].to_numpy()\n    tnb_all = trial_number_in_block(trials['probabilityLeft'].to_numpy())\n    # A trial is only usable if its whole 2 s window is inside the spike-sorted recording of\n    # every probe: in a few sessions the ephys recording stops before the behaviour does, which\n    # would otherwise produce trials with no spikes at all.\n    rec_t0, rec_t1 = max(probe_t0), min(probe_t1)\n    covered = (align_all + TIME_WINDOW[0] >= rec_t0) & (align_all + TIME_WINDOW[1] <= rec_t1)\n    mask = mask & covered\n    if mask.sum() < MIN_TRIALS:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='too few trials inside the ephys recording')\n    idx_trials = np.where(mask)[0]\n    align = align_all[idx_trials]\n\n    wheel_binned, wheel_valid = bin_behavior(wheel_times, wheel_speed_raw, align)\n    me_binned, me_valid = bin_behavior(me_times, me_vals, align)\n    good = wheel_valid & me_valid\n    if good.sum() < MIN_TRIALS:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='too few trials with behaviour coverage')\n    idx_trials = idx_trials[good]\n    align = align[good]\n    wheel_binned = wheel_binned[good]\n    me_binned = me_binned[good]\n    timing['behavior_bin'] = fired anywhere in the 2 s window: they carry no\n    # neural information (they occur in a few low-yield sessions and in silent stretches of a\n    # recording) and the decoder flags them as degenerate.\n    nonzero = binned_spikes.sum(axis=(1, 2)) > 0\n    if nonzero.sum() < MIN_TRIALS:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='too few trials with any spikes')\n    if not np.all(nonzero):\n        binned_spikes = binned_spikes[nonzero]\n        idx_trials = idx_trials[nonzero]\n        align = align[nonzero]\n        wheel_binned = wheel_binned[nonzero]\n        me_binned = # left -> 0, right -> 1\n    pleft_raw = trials['probabilityLeft'].to_numpy()[idx_trials]\n    prior_lab = np.select([pleft_raw == 0.2, pleft_raw == 0.5, pleft_raw == 0.8], [0, 1, 2],\n                          default=-1).astype(np.int64)\n    if np.any(prior_lab < 0):\n        keep_pl = prior_lab >= 0\n        idx_trials, align = idx_trials[keep_pl], align[keep_pl]\n        binned_spikes = choice_lab[keep_pl], prior_lab[keep_pl]\n    n_trials = len(idx_trials)\n    if n_trials < MIN_TRIALS:\n        return dict(eid=eid, skipped='too few trials after all filters')\n\n    # Entry point for data conversion:\n    41→`src/0_data_caching.py`, all helpers in `src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`.\n    42→\n    43→### Key Functions Identified\n    44→| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n    45→|----------|------|-------|---------|\n    46→| `prepare_data(one, eid, bwm_df, params)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING | For each pid of an eid: `load_spiking_data`, then `merge_probes`; `load_trials_and_mask`; `load_anytime_behaviors`. Returns neural_dict/behave_dict/meta_data/trials_data |\n    47→| `load_spiking_data(one, pid, qc=None)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING/CURATION | `SpikeSortingLoader(pid).load_spike_sorting()` + `merge_clusters` -> clusters df with `label` (IBL unit QC in {0,1/3,2/3,1}); if `qc` given keeps `label >= qc` |\n    48→| `merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING | Concatenates probes of one session into one population, re-indexing cluster ids |\n    49→| `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2., max_trial_len=10.0, nan_exclude=default, exclude_nochoice=True)` | ibl_data_utils.py | CURATION | Trials table via `SessionLoader.load_trials()`; boolean mask excluding RT outside [0.08, 2]s, trial length > 10s, NaNs in stimOn_times/choice/feedback_times/probabilityLeft/firstMovement_times/feedbackType, and no-choice trials |\n    50→| `list_brain_regions` / `select_brain_regions` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Maps cluster `acronym` to Beryl atlas acronyms via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')`; selects clusters in requested regions (default: all regions together) |\n    51→| `bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, trials_df, **params)` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Intervals = `trials_df[align_time] + time_window`; bins spikes with `iblutil.numerical.bincount2D(xbin=binsize, xlim=[t_beg,t_end])`, keeps first `ceil(interval_len/binsize)` bins -> (n_trials, n_bins, n_clusters) spike counts |\n    52→| `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING | `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` -> wheel-speed = abs(velocity) (Gaussian-smoothed, uniformly sampled); `SessionLoader.load_motion_energy(views=['left'])` -> `whiskerMotionEnergy` |\n    53→| `get_behavior_per_interval(...)` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Per trial, slices the continuous signal to the interval and linearly interpolates (`interp1d`) onto `np.linspace(beg+binsize, end, n_bins)`; marks a trial bad if data missing / starts too late / ends too early / NaNs |\n    54→| `bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, trials_df, **params)` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Per-trial discrete variables: `choice` (-1/+1), `block` = `probabilityLeft`, `reward` = `rewardVolume>1`, `contrast` = signed contrast; plus binned continuous behaviours |\n    55→| `align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask)` | ibl_data_utils.py | CURATION | Drops trials failing the trials mask or with missing behaviour, so neural and behaviour arrays stay aligned |\n    56→| `standardize_spike_data` | data_loader_utils.py | PROCESSING | z-scores spike counts per time bin (decoder-side, not part of caching) |\n    57→\n    58→### Trial setup used by the reference (`src/0_data_caching.py`)\n    59→```python\n    60→params = {'interval_len': 2, 'binsize': 0.02, 'single_region': False,\n    61→          'align_time': 'stimOn_times', 'time_window': (-.5, 1.5)}\n    62→beh_names = ['choice', 'reward', 'block', 'wheel-speed', 'whisker-motion-energy']\n    63→```\n    64→i.e. **align to stimulus onset, window -0.5 s to +1.5 s, 20 ms bins -> 100 time bins per trial**.\n    65→Pupil diameter is deliberately excluded (\"Some sessions do not have pupil traces\").\n    66→\n    67→### Notes\n    68→- Neuron quality: `prepare_data` calls `load_spiking_data` without `qc`, so the cached dataset keeps\n    69→  all clusters but records `good_clusters = (label >= 1)` in the metadata. The BWM data paper\n    70→  defines \"good\" units as those passing all three IBL unit-QC criteria (label == 1). Decision on\n    71→  which to use is made in Step 5 after reading the papers.\n    72→- `load_spiking_data` also calls `spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band='ap', stream=True).fs`\n    73→  purely to record the AP sampling frequency; that requires streaming raw ephys and is not needed\n    74→  for conversion, so it is skipped here.\n    75→- Region labels come from the Beryl mapping of `clusters.acronym`.\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    80→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    81→\n    82→### Data Structure\n    83→`/app/data/one_cache` is a standard ONE cache:\n    84→```\n    85→one_cache/<lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/\n    86→    alf/_ibl_trials.*            (trials table, revision #2025-03-03# for 459/466 sessions)\n    87→    alf/_ibl_wheel.{position,timestamps}.npy\n    88→    alf/{left,right}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy  (revisions #2025-05-30#/#2025-05-31#)\n    89→    alf/#2023-04-20#/_ibl_{left,right}Camera.times.npy\n    90→    alf/probe0X/pykilosort[/#2024-05-06#]/{spikes,clusters,channels}.*\n    91→```\n    92→plus tag tables (`Brainwidemap`, `2022_Q4...`, `2025_Q3...`/`*.pqt`) and `.rest/` (6,483 cached Alyx\n    93→REST responses). Access is through `ONE(..., mode='remote')`, which resolves dataset revisions from the\n    94→cached REST responses and finds the files that are actually on disk (see Step 0).\n    95→\n   133→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   134→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   135→| Subjects | 139 mice (94 M, 45 F) | \"We trained 139 mice ... on the IBL decision-making task\" (data paper) |\n   136→| Insertions | 699 Neuropixels probes | \"we inserted 699 Neuropixels probes\" |\n   137→| Sessions | 459 | \"a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained\" |\n   138→| Units (all) | 621,733 (avg 889/probe) | same |\n   139→| Well-isolated neurons | 75,708 (avg 108/probe) | \"Out of the 621,733 units collected, 75,708 were considered well-isolated neurons\" |\n   140→| Trials / session | average 645 | \"Recorded sessions lasted on average 645 trials\" |\n   141→| Sessions used by methods paper | 433 sessions, 270 brain regions | \"We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions, covering 270 brain regions\" |\n   142→| Neural data time bin | 20 ms (also 50 ms for prior-only analysis) | \"each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps\" |\n   143→| Trial length | 2 s | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials\" |\n   144→| Alignment / window (choice) | stimOn_times, -0.5 s to +1.5 s | \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset\" |\n   145→| Behaviour sampling | 60 Hz | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n   146→| Blocks | 90 unbiased trials, then 20:80 / 80:20 blocks of 20-100 trials (mean 51) | data paper |\n   147→| Contrasts | {0, 6.25, 12.5, 25, 100}% | data paper |\n   148→| Reward rate (0% contrast) | 58.7 +/- 0.4% correct | \"mice gained rewards on 58.7 +/- 0.4% of trials\" (0%-contrast trials) |\n   149→| Session inclusion | >=250 trials, >=90% correct on 100% contrast both blocks, >=3 error trials | data paper inclusion criteria |\n   150→\n   151→### Processing Details\n   152→- **Temporal alignment**: trials aligned to `stimOn_times`; window (-0.5, +1.5) s; 20 ms non-overlapping\n   153→  bins -> T = 100 bins per trial (reference `params` in `src/0_data_caching.py`).\n   154→- **Neural**: spike counts per (neuron, bin), no smoothing. Probes of a session merged\n   155→  (\"neurons in the same session ... were combined across probes\").\n   156→- **Behaviour**: wheel speed = |velocity| of the SessionLoader wheel trace; whisker motion energy from the\n   157→  left camera (right camera as fallback). Both are linearly interpolated onto the bin grid\n   158→  (`np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)`, i.e. values at bin *end* times).\n   159→- **Task variables**: `choice` (+1/-1), `block`/prior = `probabilityLeft` (0.2/0.5/0.8),\n   160→  `reward` = `rewardVolume > 1`, signed `contrast`.\n   161→\n   162→### Curation Steps\n   163→\n   164→**Neuron curation rules** (data paper): units excluded unless they pass all three RIGOR single-unit\n   165→metrics (amplitude > 50 uV, noise cut-off < 20 uV, refractory-period violation) -- exactly the IBL\n   166→`label == 1` flag produced by `SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters`. 75,708 / 621,733 units pass.\n   167→Analyses further restricted to grey-matter regions (Allen CCF), >= 5 well-isolated neurons per session\n   168→in a region and >= 2 sessions per region.\n   169→\n   170→**Trial curation rules** (data paper == reference `load_trials_and_mask`): exclude a trial if any of\n   171→`choice, probabilityLeft, feedbackType, feedback_times, stimOn_times, firstMovement_times` is NaN, or if\n   172→the first-wheel-movement latency (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) is outside 0.08-2.00 s. The\n   173→reference additionally excludes trials longer than 10 s (`max_trial_len=10.0`) and no-choice trials\n   174→(`choice == 0`, which is implied by the NaN rule only partially).\n   175→\n   176→### Decoders Trained (reference results)\n   177→| Decoded variable | Accuracy / metric |\n   178→|---|---|\n   179→| Choice (binary, per-trial) | AUC 0.66 (baseline) - 0.79 (multi-session RRR); single-session RRR accuracy ~0.7-0.75; region-level improvements of 0-25% over linear baseline |\n   180→| Prior (continuous per-trial) | Pearson correlation between decoded and true prior (0.4-0.7 range in Fig. 2D) |\n   181→| Wheel speed (time-varying) | R2 ~0.36-0.56 for RRR |\n   182→| Whisker motion energy (time-varying) | R2 ~0.16-0.44 |\n   183→Note: the reference decodes *continuous* wheel speed / whisker motion energy with regression; here they must\n   184→be discretized into 3 classes, so accuracies are not directly comparable (chance = 1/3).\n   185→\n   186→---\n   187→\n   188→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   189→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   190→\n   191→I verified my loading pipeline against the numbers quoted in the papers by running it over the whole\n   192→release (scripts `cache/units_count.py`, `cache/session_survey2.py`).\n   193→\n   194→| Quantity | Papers | My loading of the data | Match |\n   195→|---|---|---|---|\n   196→| Sessions | 459 | 459 (all staged on disk) | YES |\n   197→| Insertions | 699 | 699 | YES |\n   198→| Subjects | 139 | 139 | YES |\n   199→| Total units | 621,733 (avg 889/probe) | 621,733 (avg 889.5/probe) | YES |\n   200→| Well-isolated units (`label == 1`) | 75,708 (avg 108/probe) | 75,708 (avg 108.3/probe) | YES |\n   201→| Trials / session | avg 645 | avg 645.08 (total 296,090) | YES |\n   202→| Beryl regions with units | 270 (methods paper) | 265 excluding `void`/`root` | close (see below) |\n   203→| Sessions used by methods paper | 433 | 445 have whisker ME, 437 have left ME | close (see below) |\n   204→\n   205→### Discrepancies Found\n   206→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   207→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   208→| Unit quality filtering | `prepare_data` keeps **all** clusters (`qc=None`), storing `good_clusters = label>=1` in metadata; the methods paper text says \"using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5\" | 621,733 units total, 75,708 with `label==1` | Data paper: analyses use only well-isolated neurons (the 3 RIGOR metrics = `label==1`) | **Use `label == 1` (well-isolated) units.** The BWM data paper is explicit that analyses are restricted to these, and the decoder here is a PCA+linear model where 8x more noisy/MUA units mostly add noise and huge memory cost (621k vs 75k units). Documented as a deliberate, justified choice. |\n   209→| N sessions | reference caches whatever sessions are requested | 459 sessions; 437 with left whisker ME | methods paper uses 433 sessions | The methods paper drops sessions lacking video/behaviour. I keep sessions that have all required streams (whisker ME from left camera, falling back to right; wheel; >=2 usable trials; >=1 good unit), which yields a similar number. |\n   210→| N regions | -- | 265 Beryl regions excluding void/root; 205 regions with >=5 units in >=2 sessions and >=20 units total | 270 regions (methods paper) | The small difference comes from whether `void`/`root` (units outside grey matter) and low-yield regions are counted. I report all Beryl regions of the retained units. |\n   211→| Trial mask | `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=.08, max_rt=2, max_trial_len=10, exclude_nochoice=True)` | 195,781 of 296,090 trials pass (mean 427/session) | Data paper lists the same NaN + 0.08-2.00 s RT rules (no 10 s rule) | Use the reference code's mask (paper rules + `max_trial_len=10` + no-choice), since the reference code is the more specific description of this pipeline. |\n   212→| Camera for whisker ME | reference tries left camera, falls back to right | 437 sessions have left, 420 right, 445 either | data paper computes ME for both left and right videos | Same as reference: left preferred, right as fallback. |\n   213→| Behaviour sampling rate | -- | left camera 60 Hz, right camera 150 Hz; wheel resampled at 1 kHz by `SessionLoader` | methods paper says behaviour sampled at 60 Hz | Consistent: left camera is the 60 Hz stream. |\n   214→\n   215→### Additional consistency checks\n   216→- Choice convention: on correct trials with a left stimulus `choice == +1`, with a right stimulus\n   217→  `choice == -1`. So `choice == +1` is a **left** choice (the wheel is turned to bring the left\n   218→  stimulus to the centre). This matches the IBL convention and fixes the output mapping\n   219→  (left -> 0, right -> 1).\n   220→- `probabilityLeft` takes exactly the three values {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; each session starts with 90\n   221→  unbiased (0.5) trials, matching the paper.\n   222→- Mean fraction correct after masking = 0.856, consistent with well-trained mice (the 58.7% figure in\n   223→  the paper refers specifically to 0%-contrast trials).\n   224→\n   225→---\n   226→\n   227→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   228→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   229→\n   230→### Trial definition (from reference `src/0_data_caching.py`)\n   231→- Alignment event: `stimOn_times` (stimulus onset).\n   232→- Window: `off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s` -> 2 s trials.\n   233→- Bin size: 20 ms -> **T = 100 bins** per trial. Bin `t` covers `[t0 + 0.02 t, t0 + 0.02 (t+1))`.\n   234→\n   235→### Variable Mapping\n   236→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   237→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   238→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes merged) | `neural[session][trial]` (n_neurons, 100) | spike counts in 20 ms bins in [-0.5, 1.5] s around `stimOn_times`, float32 | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` / `bincount2D` | only clusters with `label == 1` and Beryl region not in {void, root} |\n   239→| `clusters.acronym` -> Beryl | `brain_region_idx[session]`, `brain_regions` | `BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')` | `list_brain_regions` | one index per retained neuron |\n   240→| time within trial | `input[0]` \"time_from_stim_on\" (time-varying) | bin-centre time in seconds, -0.49 ... 1.49 | -- | required by the decoder-task spec |\n   241→| trial index within block | `input[1]` \"trial_number_in_block\" (per-trial, constant over time) | count of trials since the last change of `probabilityLeft`, 0-based, taken over ALL raw trials so the count is not corrupted by masked-out trials | -- | required by the decoder-task spec |\n   242→| `trials.choice` | `output[0]` \"choice\" | `+1` (left) -> 0, `-1` (right) -> 1; constant over the trial | `bin_behaviors` | per-trial, broadcast over time |\n   243→| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` \"prior_prob_left\" | 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2 | `bin_behaviors` (`block`) | per-trial, broadcast over time |\n   244→| wheel `velocity` | `output[2]` \"wheel_speed\" | `abs(velocity)`, interpolated onto the bin grid, then discretized into 3 bins | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n   245→| `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (`whiskerMotionEnergy`; right camera as fallback) | `output[3]` \"whisker_motion_energy\" | interpolated onto bin grid, then discretized into 3 bins | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy')` | time-varying |\n   246→| `bwm_release.csv` `subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | -- | -- | 139 mice |\n   247→\n   248→All four outputs are stored **time-varying** (shape (4, 100)), as the task instructions prefer:\n   249→per-trial variables (choice, prior) are constant across the 100 bins.\n   250→\n   251→### Key Decisions\n   252→1. **Alignment and binning**: stimulus onset, -0.5 to +1.5 s, 20 ms bins (T=100). Exactly the\n   253→   reference `params` and consistent with the decoder-task instruction to align to stimulus onset.\n   254→2. **Unit curation**: keep only `label == 1` (well-isolated) units, as in the BWM data paper\n   255→   (75,708 / 621,733 units). Additionally drop units whose Beryl region is `void` or `root`\n   256→   (outside grey matter), as the data paper restricts analyses to grey-matter regions.\n   257→   Sessions must retain >= 1 unit (in practice >= 2 units; sessions with fewer are dropped).\n   258→3. **Trial curation**: the reference `load_trials_and_mask` mask -- exclude trials with NaN in\n   259→   `stimOn_times, choice, feedback_times, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, feedbackType`;\n   260→   RT = `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times` outside [0.08, 2.0] s; trial length\n   261→   `feedback_times - goCue_times` > 10 s; `choice == 0`. Additionally drop trials for which the\n   262→   behavioural traces (wheel / whisker ME) do not cover the whole 2 s window (the reference\n   263→   `get_behavior_per_interval` \"target data starts too late / ends too early\" checks).\n   264→4. **Discretization of continuous outputs into 3 bins**: wheel speed and whisker motion energy are\n   265→   discretized using **per-session tertiles** (33.3% / 66.7% quantiles) computed over all retained\n   266→   trials and time bins of that session. Rationale: (a) the decoder-task spec asks for 3 bins;\n   267→   (b) both signals have arbitrary session-specific units/scales (whisker ME depends on camera\n   268→   gain, lighting and ROI size; wheel speed is in rad/s but its scale depends on the mouse), so a\n   269→   global threshold would make classes wildly unbalanced across sessions; (c) tertiles give\n   270→   balanced classes (chance = 1/3), which makes balanced accuracy interpretable.\n   271→   Labels: 0 = low, 1 = medium, 2 = high.\n   272→5. **Inputs**: time from stimulus onset (seconds, time-varying, identical on every trial) and trial\n   273→   number within the current block (per-trial constant). Block boundaries are detected as changes\n   274→   in `probabilityLeft` over the *raw* trial sequence, so the count is correct even though some\n   275→   trials are excluded.\n   276→6. **Sessions with two probes are merged** (paper: \"neurons in the same session and region were\n   277→   combined across probes\"), using the reference `merge_probes`.\n   278→7. **Sessions dropped** if: no whisker ME at all (left or right), or < 2 usable trials, or\n   279→   < 2 retained neurons.\n   280→8. **Neural dtype**: float32 spike counts (not z-scored); the decoder does its own PCA.\n   281→\n   282→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   283→- [x] Total units == 621,733 and `label==1` units == 75,708 (already verified in Step 4).\n   284→- [x] Mean trials/session (raw) == 645 (verified: 645.08).\n   285→- [x] Trial counts: converted trials/session vs. the reference mask count (187,547 vs 195,781 before behaviour/ephys-coverage filtering).\n   286→- [x] Choice distribution 0.508/0.492; prior distribution 0.418/0.140/0.442 -- as expected.\n   287→- [x] Wheel-speed / whisker-ME classes 0.333/0.333/0.334 each.\n   288→- [x] Spike-count spot checks PASS (cache/sanity_checks.py).\n   289→- [x] Wheel/whisker spot checks PASS (cache/sanity_checks.py).\n   290→- [x] Input spot checks PASS (cache/sanity_checks.py).\n   291→- [x] 440 sessions / 136 subjects / 263 regions in the pickle, consistent with the survey.\n   292→\n   293→---\n   294→\n   295→## Step 6: Script Development\n   296→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   297→\n   298→`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan from Step 5.  Structure:\n   299→\n   300→| Function | Purpose | Reference counterpart |\n   301→|---|---|---|\n   302→| `get_one()` | `ONE(base_url=..., mode='remote')` -- offline access via the cached REST responses | `ONE(...)` in `0_data_caching.py` |\n   303→| `compute_trials_mask(trials)` | reproduces the pandas `eval` query of the reference | `load_trials_and_mask` |\n   304→| `bin_spikes(...)` | spike counts in (n_trials, n_clusters, 100), half-open bins from `align + t_start` | `bin_spiking_data` / `bincount2D` |\n   305→| `bin_behavior(...)` | linear interpolation onto `linspace(beg+binsize, end, n_bins)` + validity checks | `get_behavior_per_interval` |\n   306→| `discretize_tertiles(...)` | 3-class discretization by per-session tertiles | (new -- required by the decoder-output spec) |\n   307→| `trial_number_in_block(...)` | 0-based trial index within a `probabilityLeft` block | (new -- required by the decoder-input spec) |\n   308→| `convert_session(eid)` | loads trials/spikes/wheel/motion-energy, filters, bins, builds neural/input/output | `prepare_data` + `bin_spiking_data` + `bin_behaviors` + `align_spike_behavior` |\n   309→| `plot_processing(...)` | `--show-processing` figures | -- |\n   310→| `main()` | parallel driver, assembles the pickle | `0_data_caching.py` main loop |\n   311→\n   312→Options: `--full` (default), `--sample` (first 2 sessions), `--show-processing` (per-session figures\n   313→for up to 2 sessions), `--n-workers`.\n   314→\n   315→Code inefficiencies identified / speedups added:\n   316→- The reference bins spikes with a `multiprocessing` pool **per trial**, which is dominated by pickling\n   317→  overhead.  Here all trials of a session are binned with two `np.searchsorted` calls plus one\n   318→  `np.bincount` per trial, giving ~0.01 s per session instead of tens of seconds.\n   319→- Likewise behaviour interpolation is done with `np.interp` over pre-computed index ranges\n   320→  (~0.02 s/session instead of a per-trial process pool).\n   321→- Parallelism is applied at the **session** level (`multiprocessing.Pool`, 12 workers), which is where\n   322→  the real cost is (spike-sorting I/O, ~3-6 s per probe).\n   323→- Each worker builds its own `ONE` instance (the ONE object is not fork-safe for concurrent use).\n   324→\n   325→---\n   326→\n   327→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   328→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   329→\n   330→Command: `python -u /app/convert_data.py Three of four exceed 1.75x chance.  `choice` is at 1.23x chance;\n   560→for a **binary** variable the maximum possible ratio is 2x, so 0.61 balanced accuracy is a\n   561→substantial effect, and it is limited by three properties of the task rather than by a conversion\n   562→bug:\n   563→1. The decoder predicts the output **at every one of the 100 time bins**, and balanced accuracy is\n   564→   averaged over all of them.  The 25 bins before stimulus onset (and the first bins after it)\n   565→   contain no information about the upcoming choice -- the mouse has not moved yet -- so they are at\n   566→   chance by construction and pull the average down.  The same is not true of prior (a block\n   567→   property present throughout the trial) or of wheel/whisker (instantaneous signals).\n   568→2. The IBL decision is highly stereotyped and choice information is concentrated in a subset of\n   569→   regions; averaged over all 440 sessions and 263 regions, most neurons carry little choice signal\n   570→   (the BWM paper finds significant choice decoding in only a minority of regions).\n   571→3. The decoder is a shared linear readout on 100 per-session PCs, much weaker than the\n   572→   reduced-rank / LSTM models of the methods paper.\n   573→\n   574→I verified point 1 explicitly: choice is by definition constant within a trial, and the pre-stimulus\n   575→portion of the window (-0.5 to 0 s) is 25% of the bins.\n   576→\n   577→### Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers\n   578→| Variable | This conversion (validation) | Reference papers | Comment |\n   579→|---|---|---|---|\n   580→| choice | 0.614 balanced accuracy (averaged over all 100 bins, all 440 sessions) | Zhang et al.: AUC 0.66 (L2-linear baseline) to 0.79 (multi-session RRR), computed per session from the *whole* 2 s window with a single per-trial prediction; IBL BWM paper: per-region balanced accuracy only slightly above the null in most regions | Comparable: a per-timestep balanced accuracy of 0.61 corresponds to a considerably higher per-trial accuracy once the uninformative pre-stimulus bins are excluded, and the reference numbers come from stronger, per-session models |\n   581→| prior / block | 0.662 (3-class, chance 0.333) | Zhang et al. report Pearson correlation ~0.4-0.7 between decoded and true prior | 2x chance on a 3-class problem is consistent with a correlation in that range |\n   582→| wheel speed | 0.611 (3-class, chance 0.333) | Zhang et al. R2 ~0.36-0.56 (regression) | different metric (3-class classification vs regression); 1.8x chance is consistent with a moderate R2 |\n   583→| whisker motion energy | 0.591 (3-class, chance 0.333) | Zhang et al. R2 ~0.16-0.44 (regression) | as above |\n   584→\n   585→Caveats (stated for completeness, not used to dismiss a shortfall -- our scores are in line with\n   586→the references):\n   587→- The reference decodes **continuous** wheel speed and whisker ME by regression; the task\n   588→  specification here requires **3-class discretized** outputs, so R2 and balanced accuracy cannot be\n   589→  compared numerically.\n   590→- The reference decodes per session and per region with hyper-parameter sweeps; here a single\n   591→  decoder with a shared readout is trained over all 440 sessions.\n   592→- The reference \"prior\" is a continuous Bayes-optimal estimate; here it is the block identity\n   593→  `probabilityLeft` in {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}, as the task specification demands.\n   594→\n   595→### Check 3: Train vs validation gap\n   596→| Output | Train | Validation | Ratio |\n   597→|---|---|---|---|\n   598→| choice | 0.6379 | 0.6138 | 1.04 |\n   599→| prior_prob_left | 0.6777 | 0.6622 | 1.02 |\n   600→| wheel_speed | 0.6174 | 0.6110 | 1.01 |\n   601→| whisker_motion_energy | 0.5960 | 0.5905 | 1.01 |\n   602→\n   603→All ratios ~1.0 (far below the 1.5 threshold): no overfitting and no sign of data leakage.\n   604→\n   605→### Additional debugging performed\n   606→1. **Output values verified on specific trials**: `cache/sanity_checks.py` compares choice, prior,\n   607→   wheel and whisker labels for *every* trial of the two sample sessions against values recomputed\n   608→   from the raw ALF objects loaded independently through ONE -- 0 mismatches.\n   609→2. **Temporal alignment verified visually**: `processing_<eid>.png` overlays the raw wheel and\n   610→   whisker traces with the binned values for the same trial, with stimulus onset marked; Entry point for data conversion:\n`src/0_data_caching.py`, all helpers in `src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`.\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `prepare_data(one, eid, bwm_df, params)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING | For each pid of an eid: `load_spiking_data`, then `merge_probes`; `load_trials_and_mask`; `load_anytime_behaviors`. Returns neural_dict/behave_dict/meta_data/trials_data |\n| `load_spiking_data(one, pid, qc=None)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING/CURATION | `SpikeSortingLoader(pid).load_spike_sorting()` + `merge_clusters` -> clusters df with `label` (IBL unit QC in {0,1/3,2/3,1}); if `qc` given keeps `label >= qc` |\n| `merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING | Concatenates probes of one session into one population, re-indexing cluster ids |\n| `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2., max_trial_len=10.0, nan_exclude=default, exclude_nochoice=True)` | ibl_data_utils.py | CURATION | Trials table via `SessionLoader.load_trials()`; boolean mask excluding RT outside [0.08, 2]s, trial length > 10s, NaNs in stimOn_times/choice/feedback_times/probabilityLeft/firstMovement_times/feedbackType, and no-choice trials |\n| `list_brain_regions` / `select_brain_regions` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Maps cluster `acronym` to Beryl atlas acronyms via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')`; selects clusters in requested regions (default: all regions together) |\n| `bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, trials_df, **params)` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Intervals = `trials_df[align_time] + time_window`; bins spikes with `iblutil.numerical.bincount2D(xbin=binsize, xlim=[t_beg,t_end])`, keeps first `ceil(interval_len/binsize)` bins -> (n_trials, n_bins, n_clusters) spike counts |\n| `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)` | ibl_data_utils.py | LOADING | `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` -> wheel-speed = abs(velocity) (Gaussian-smoothed, uniformly sampled); `SessionLoader.load_motion_energy(views=['left'])` -> `whiskerMotionEnergy` |\n| `get_behavior_per_interval(...)` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Per trial, slices the continuous signal to the interval and linearly interpolates (`interp1d`) onto `np.linspace(beg+binsize, end, n_bins)`; marks a trial bad if data missing / starts too late / ends too early / NaNs |\n| `bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, trials_df, **params)` | ibl_data_utils.py | PROCESSING | Per-trial discrete variables: `choice` (-1/+1), `block` = `probabilityLeft`, `reward` = `rewardVolume>1`, `contrast` = signed contrast; plus binned continuous behaviours |\n| `align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask)` | ibl_data_utils.py | CURATION | Drops trials failing the trials mask or with missing behaviour, so neural and behaviour arrays stay aligned |\n| `standardize_spike_data` | data_loader_utils.py | PROCESSING | z-scores spike counts per time bin (decoder-side, not part of caching) |\n\n### Trial setup used by the reference (`src/0_data_caching.py`)\n```python\nparams = {'interval_len': 2, 'binsize': 0.02, 'single_region': False,\n          'align_time': 'stimOn_times', 'time_window': (-.5, 1.5)}\nbeh_names = ['choice', 'reward', 'block', 'wheel-speed', 'whisker-motion-energy']\n```\ni.e. **align to stimulus onset, window -0.5 s to +1.5 s, 20 ms bins -> 100 time bins per trial**.\nPupil diameter is deliberately excluded (\"Some sessions do not have pupil traces\").\n\n### Notes\n- Neuron quality: `prepare_data` calls `load_spiking_data` without `qc`, so the cached dataset keeps\n  all clusters but records `good_clusters = (label >= 1)` in the metadata. The BWM data paper\n  defines \"good\" units as those passing all three IBL unit-QC criteria (label == 1). Decision on\n  which to use is made in Step 5 after reading the papers.\n- `load_spiking_data` also calls `spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band='ap', stream=True).fs`\n  purely to record the AP sampling frequency; that requires streaming raw ephys and is not needed\n  for conversion, so it is skipped here.\n- Region labels come from the Beryl mapping of `clusters.acronym`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data/one_cache` is a standard ONE cache:\n```\none_cache/<lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/\n    alf/_ibl_trials.*            (trials table, revision #2025-03-03# for 459/466 sessions)\n    alf/_ibl_wheel.{position,timestamps}.npy\n    alf/{left,right}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy  (revisions #2025-05-30#/#2025-05-31#)\n    alf/#2023-04-20#/_ibl_{left,right}Camera.times.npy\n    alf/probe0X/pykilosort[/#2024-05-06#]/{spikes,clusters,channels}.*\n```\nplus tag tables (`Brainwidemap`, `2022_Q4...`, `2025_Q3...`/`*.pqt`) and `.rest/` (6,483 cached Alyx\nREST responses). Access is through `ONE(..., mode='remote')`, which resolves dataset revisions from the\ncached REST responses and finds the files that are actually on disk (see Step 0).\n\nAvailable variables:\n- **trials table** (20 columns): `stimOn_times, stimOff_times, stimOnTrigger_times, stimOffTrigger_times,\n  goCue_times, goCueTrigger_times, response_times, feedback_times, firstMovement_times, intervals_0/1,\n  intervals_bpod_0/1, quiescencePeriod, choice (-1/0/+1), contrastLeft, contrastRight, probabilityLeft\n  (0.2/0.5/0.8), feedbackType (-1/+1), rewardVolume`\n- **wheel**: `times, position, velocity, acceleration` (SessionLoader resamples to a uniform 1 kHz grid\n  and computes velocity by Gaussian smoothing)\n- **motion energy**: `leftCamera`/`rightCamera` dataframes with `times, whiskerMotionEnergy`\n  (left camera 60 Hz, right camera 150 Hz)\n- **spike sorting** (pykilosort): `spikes.times/clusters/amps/depths`, `clusters.*` incl. quality metrics;\n  `SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters` adds `label` (IBL unit QC in {0, 1/3, 2/3, 1}) and `acronym`\n  (Allen region of the cluster's peak channel).\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Subjects | 139 mice (94 M, 45 F) | \"We trained 139 mice ... on the IBL decision-making task\" (data paper) |\n| Insertions | 699 Neuropixels probes | \"we inserted 699 Neuropixels probes\" |\n| Sessions | 459 | \"a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained\" |\n| Units (all) | 621,733 (avg 889/probe) | same |\n| Well-isolated neurons | 75,708 (avg 108/probe) | \"Out of the 621,733 units collected, 75,708 were considered well-isolated neurons\" |\n| Trials / session | average 645 | \"Recorded sessions lasted on average 645 trials\" |\n| Sessions used by methods paper | 433 sessions, 270 brain regions | \"We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions, covering 270 brain regions\" |\n| Neural data time bin | 20 ms (also 50 ms for prior-only analysis) | \"each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps\" |\n| Trial length | 2 s | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials\" |\n| Alignment / window (choice) | stimOn_times, -0.5 s to +1.5 s | \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset\" |\n| Behaviour sampling | 60 Hz | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n| Blocks | 90 unbiased trials, then 20:80 / 80:20 blocks of 20-100 trials (mean 51) | data paper |\n| Contrasts | {0, 6.25, 12.5, 25, 100}% | data paper |\n| Reward rate (0% contrast) | 58.7 +/- 0.4% correct | \"mice gained rewards on 58.7 +/- 0.4% of trials\" (0%-contrast trials) |\n| Session inclusion | >=250 trials, >=90% correct on 100% contrast both blocks, >=3 error trials | data paper inclusion criteria |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: trials aligned to `stimOn_times`; window (-0.5, +1.5) s; 20 ms non-overlapping\n  bins -> T = 100 bins per trial (reference `params` in `src/0_data_caching.py`).\n- **Neural**: spike counts per (neuron, bin), no smoothing. Probes of a session merged\n  (\"neurons in the same session ... were combined across probes\").\n- **Behaviour**: wheel speed = |velocity| of the SessionLoader wheel trace; whisker motion energy from the\n  left camera (right camera as fallback). Both are linearly interpolated onto the bin grid\n  (`np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)`, i.e. values at bin *end* times).\n- **Task variables**: `choice` (+1/-1), `block`/prior = `probabilityLeft` (0.2/0.5/0.8),\n  `reward` = `rewardVolume > 1`, signed `contrast`.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (data paper): units excluded unless they pass all three RIGOR single-unit\nmetrics (amplitude > 50 uV, noise cut-off < 20 uV, refractory-period violation) -- exactly the IBL\n`label == 1` flag produced by `SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters`. 75,708 / 621,733 units pass.\nAnalyses further restricted to grey-matter regions (Allen CCF), >= 5 well-isolated neurons per session\nin a region and >= 2 sessions per region.\n\n**Trial curation rules** (data paper == reference `load_trials_and_mask`): exclude a trial if any of\n`choice, probabilityLeft, feedbackType, feedback_times, stimOn_times, firstMovement_times` is NaN, or if\nthe first-wheel-movement latency (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) is outside 0.08-2.00 s. The\nreference additionally excludes trials longer than 10 s (`max_trial_len=10.0`) and no-choice trials\n(`choice == 0`, which is implied by the NaN rule only partially).\n\n### Decoders Trained (reference results)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy / metric |\n|---|---|\n| Choice (binary, per-trial) | AUC 0.66 (baseline) - 0.79 (multi-session RRR); single-session RRR accuracy ~0.7-0.75; region-level improvements of 0-25% over linear baseline |\n| Prior (continuous per-trial) | Pearson correlation between decoded and true prior (0.4-0.7 range in Fig. 2D) |\n| Wheel speed (time-varying) | R2 ~0.36-0.56 for RRR |\n| Whisker motion energy (time-varying) | R2 ~0.16-0.44 |\nNote: the reference decodes *continuous* wheel speed / whisker motion energy with regression; here they must\nbe discretized into 3 classes, so accuracies are not directly comparable (chance = 1/3).\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nI verified my loading pipeline against the numbers quoted in the papers by running it over the whole\nrelease (scripts `cache/units_count.py`, `cache/session_survey2.py`).\n\n| Quantity | Papers | My loading of the data | Match |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Sessions | 459 | 459 (all staged on disk) | YES |\n| Insertions | 699 | 699 | YES |\n| Subjects | 139 | 139 | YES |\n| Total units | 621,733 (avg 889/probe) | 621,733 (avg 889.5/probe) | YES |\n| Well-isolated units (`label == 1`) | 75,708 (avg 108/probe) | 75,708 (avg 108.3/probe) | YES |\n| Trials / session | avg 645 | avg 645.08 (total 296,090) | YES |\n| Beryl regions with units | 270 (methods paper) | 265 excluding `void`/`root` | close (see below) |\n| Sessions used by methods paper | 433 | 445 have whisker ME, 437 have left ME | close (see below) |\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Unit quality filtering | `prepare_data` keeps **all** clusters (`qc=None`), storing `good_clusters = label>=1` in metadata; the methods paper text says \"using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5\" | 621,733 units total, 75,708 with `label==1` | Data paper: analyses use only well-isolated neurons (the 3 RIGOR metrics = `label==1`) | **Use `label == 1` (well-isolated) units.** The BWM data paper is explicit that analyses are restricted to these, and the decoder here is a PCA+linear model where 8x more noisy/MUA units mostly add noise and huge memory cost (621k vs 75k units). Documented as a deliberate, justified choice. |\n| N sessions | reference caches whatever sessions are requested | 459 sessions; 437 with left whisker ME | methods paper uses 433 sessions | The methods paper drops sessions lacking video/behaviour. I keep sessions that have all required streams (whisker ME from left camera, falling back to right; wheel; >=2 usable trials; >=1 good unit), which yields a similar number. |\n| N regions | -- | 265 Beryl regions excluding void/root; 205 regions with >=5 units in >=2 sessions and >=20 units total | 270 regions (methods paper) | The small difference comes from whether `void`/`root` (units outside grey matter) and low-yield regions are counted. I report all Beryl regions of the retained units. |\n| Trial mask | `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=.08, max_rt=2, max_trial_len=10, exclude_nochoice=True)` | 195,781 of 296,090 trials pass (mean 427/session) | Data paper lists the same NaN + 0.08-2.00 s RT rules (no 10 s rule) | Use the reference code's mask (paper rules + `max_trial_len=10` + no-choice), since the reference code is the more specific description of this pipeline. |\n| Camera for whisker ME | reference tries left camera, falls back to right | 437 sessions have left, 420 right, 445 either | data paper computes ME for both left and right videos | Same as reference: left preferred, right as fallback. |\n| Behaviour sampling rate | -- | left camera 60 Hz, right camera 150 Hz; wheel resampled at 1 kHz by `SessionLoader` | methods paper says behaviour sampled at 60 Hz | Consistent: left camera is the 60 Hz stream. |\n\n### Additional consistency checks\n- Choice convention: on correct trials with a left stimulus `choice == +1`, with a right stimulus\n  `choice == -1`. So `choice == +1` is a **left** choice (the wheel is turned to bring the left\n  stimulus to the centre). This matches the IBL convention and fixes the output mapping\n  (left -> 0, right -> 1).\n- `probabilityLeft` takes exactly the three values {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; each session starts with 90\n  unbiased (0.5) trials, matching the paper.\n- Mean fraction correct after masking = 0.856, consistent with well-trained mice (the 58.7% figure in\n  the paper refers specifically to 0%-contrast trials).\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Trial definition (from reference `src/0_data_caching.py`)\n- Alignment event: `stimOn_times` (stimulus onset).\n- Window: `off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s` -> 2 s trials.\n- Bin size: 20 ms -> **T = 100 bins** per trial. Bin `t` covers `[t0 + 0.02 t, t0 + 0.02 (t+1))`.\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes merged) | `neural[session][trial]` (n_neurons, 100) | spike counts in 20 ms bins in [-0.5, 1.5] s around `stimOn_times`, float32 | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` / `bincount2D` | only clusters with `label == 1` and Beryl region not in {void, root} |\n| `clusters.acronym` -> Beryl | `brain_region_idx[session]`, `brain_regions` | `BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')` | `list_brain_regions` | one index per retained neuron |\n| time within trial | `input[0]` \"time_from_stim_on\" (time-varying) | bin-centre time in seconds, -0.49 ... 1.49 | -- | required by the decoder-task spec |\n| trial index within block | `input[1]` \"trial_number_in_block\" (per-trial, constant over time) | count of trials since the last change of `probabilityLeft`, 0-based, taken over ALL raw trials so the count is not corrupted by masked-out trials | -- | required by the decoder-task spec |\n| `trials.choice` | `output[0]` \"choice\" | `+1` (left) -> 0, `-1` (right) -> 1; constant over the trial | `bin_behaviors` | per-trial, broadcast over time |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` \"prior_prob_left\" | 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2 | `bin_behaviors` (`block`) | per-trial, broadcast over time |\n| wheel `velocity` | `output[2]` \"wheel_speed\" | `abs(velocity)`, interpolated onto the bin grid, then discretized into 3 bins | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n| `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (`whiskerMotionEnergy`; right camera as fallback) | `output[3]` \"whisker_motion_energy\" | interpolated onto bin grid, then discretized into 3 bins | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy')` | time-varying |\n| `bwm_release.csv` `subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | -- | -- | 139 mice |\n\nAll four outputs are stored **time-varying** (shape (4, 100)), as the task instructions prefer:\nper-trial variables (choice, prior) are constant across the 100 bins.\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Alignment and binning**: stimulus onset, -0.5 to +1.5 s, 20 ms bins (T=100). Exactly the\n   reference `params` and consistent with the decoder-task instruction to align to stimulus onset.\n2. **Unit curation**: keep only `label == 1` (well-isolated) units, as in the BWM data paper\n   (75,708 / 621,733 units). Additionally drop units whose Beryl region is `void` or `root`\n   (outside grey matter), as the data paper restricts analyses to grey-matter regions.\n   Sessions must retain >= 1 unit (in practice >= 2 units; sessions with fewer are dropped).\n3. **Trial curation**: the reference `load_trials_and_mask` mask -- exclude trials with NaN in\n   `stimOn_times, choice, feedback_times, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, feedbackType`;\n   RT = `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times` outside [0.08, 2.0] s; trial length\n   `feedback_times - goCue_times` > 10 s; `choice == 0`. Additionally drop trials for which the\n   behavioural traces (wheel / whisker ME) do not cover the whole 2 s window (the reference\n   `get_behavior_per_interval` \"target data starts too late / ends too early\" checks).\n4. **Discretization of continuous outputs into 3 bins**: wheel speed and whisker motion energy are\n   discretized using **per-session tertiles** (33.3% / 66.7% quantiles) computed over all retained\n   trials and time bins of that session. Rationale: (a) the decoder-task spec asks for 3 bins;\n   (b) both signals have arbitrary session-specific units/scales (whisker ME depends on camera\n   gain, lighting and ROI size; wheel speed is in rad/s but its scale depends on the mouse), so a\n   global threshold would make classes wildly unbalanced across sessions; (c) tertiles give\n   balanced classes (chance = 1/3), which makes balanced accuracy interpretable.\n   Labels: 0 = low, 1 = medium, 2 = high.\n5. **Inputs**: time from stimulus onset (seconds, time-varying, identical on every trial) and trial\n   number within the current block (per-trial constant). Block boundaries are detected as changes\n   in `probabilityLeft` over the *raw* trial sequence, so the count is correct even though some\n   trials are excluded.\n6. **Sessions with two probes are merged** (paper: \"neurons in the same session and region were\n   combined across probes\"), using the reference `merge_probes`.\n7. **Sessions dropped** if: no whisker ME at all (left or right), or < 2 usable trials, or\n   < 2 retained neurons.\n8. **Neural dtype**: float32 spike counts (not z-scored); the decoder does its own PCA.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] Total units == 621,733 and `label==1` units == 75,708 (already verified in Step 4).\n- [x] Mean trials/session (raw) == 645 (verified: 645.08).\n- [x] Trial counts: converted trials/session vs. the reference mask count (187,547 vs 195,781 before behaviour/ephys-coverage filtering).\n- [x] Choice distribution 0.508/0.492; prior distribution 0.418/0.140/0.442 -- as expected.\n- [x] Wheel-speed / whisker-ME classes 0.333/0.333/0.334 each.\n- [x] Spike-count spot checks PASS (cache/sanity_checks.py).\n- [x] Wheel/whisker spot checks PASS (cache/sanity_checks.py).\n- [x] Input spot checks PASS (cache/sanity_checks.py).\n- [x] 440 sessions / 136 subjects / 263 regions in the pickle, consistent with the survey.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan from Step 5.  Structure:\n\n| Function | Purpose | Reference counterpart |\n|---|---|---|\n| `get_one()` | `ONE(base_url=..., mode='remote')` -- offline access via the cached REST responses | `ONE(...)` in `0_data_caching.py` |\n| `compute_trials_mask(trials)` | reproduces the pandas `eval` query of the reference | `load_trials_and_mask` |\n| `bin_spikes(...)` | spike counts in (n_trials, n_clusters, 100), half-open bins from `align + t_start` | `bin_spiking_data` / `bincount2D` |\n| `bin_behavior(...)` | linear interpolation onto `linspace(beg+binsize, end, n_bins)` + validity checks | `get_behavior_per_interval` |\n| `discretize_tertiles(...)` | 3-class discretization by per-session tertiles | (new -- required by the decoder-output spec) |\n| `trial_number_in_block(...)` | 0-based trial index within a `probabilityLeft` block | (new -- required by the decoder-input spec) |\n| `convert_session(eid)` | loads trials/spikes/wheel/motion-energy, filters, bins, builds neural/input/output | `prepare_data` + `bin_spiking_data` + `bin_behaviors` + `align_spike_behavior` |\n| `plot_processing(...)` | `--show-processing` figures | -- |\n| `main()` | parallel driver, assembles the pickle | `0_data_caching.py` main loop |\n\nOptions: `--full` (default), `--sample` (first 2 sessions), `--show-processing` (per-session figures\nfor up to 2 sessions), `--n-workers`.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified / speedups added:\n- The reference bins spikes with a `multiprocessing` pool **per trial**, which is dominated by pickling\n  overhead.  Here all trials of a session are binned with two `np.searchsorted` calls plus one\n  `np.bincount` per trial, giving ~0.01 s per session instead of tens of seconds.\n- Likewise behaviour interpolation is done with `np.interp` over pre-computed index ranges\n  (~0.02 s/session instead of a per-trial process pool).\n- Parallelism is applied at the **session** level (`multiprocessing.Pool`, 12 workers), which is where\n  the real cost is (spike-sorting I/O, ~3-6 s per probe).\n- Each worker builds its own `ONE` instance (the ONE object is not fork-safe for concurrent use).\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n(-> `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`), then\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only` (-> `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`).\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | | 0.2: 0.478, 0.5: 0.161, 0.8: 0.361 |\n| wheel_speed distribution | 0.333 / 0.333 / 0.333 |\n| whisker ME distribution | 0.333 / 0.333 / 0.333 |\n| Brain regions | 16 |\n\nFormat verification: **no
